will be used to perform a
\mathtt{dB}
-step, while they decrement the second
counter to compensate for the left-hand-side value that is not being consumed
after all. In other words, consuming values are systematically typed by
incrementing their exponential counter by one (rules
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
and

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
), but they can finally act as computations instead as values,
notably when they are placed in a head position, so that their exponential
counter needs to be adjusted correctly (
cf.
Ex.
26
). The decrement
of the exponential counters in rules
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
and
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
can also
be understood by means of the subtle translation from CBV to the

\lambda!
-calculus that we introduce in Sec.
7
. Rule

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
is particularly useful to type
\mathtt{dB}
-redexes whose reduction
does not create an exponential redex, because the argument of the substitution
created by the
\mathtt{dB}
-step does not reduce to a value.

In spite of the decrements in rules
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
and
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, the
counters are positive.

Lemma 13
.

If

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

then
\mathit{e}\geq 0
. Moreover, if
\sigma
is a multitype then
\mathit{e}>0
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Phi
analysing the last rule applied.

•

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. The
result is immediate.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
,
\sigma=\mathtt{n}
,

\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
and we have type derivations
\Phi_{r}
and

\Phi_{u}
with counters
\mathit{e}_{1}
and
\mathit{e}_{2}
resp. both assigning
non-multiset types. By
i.h.
we get
\mathit{e}_{1}\geq 0
and
\mathit{e}_{2}\geq 0
, and
hence we conclude
\mathit{e}\geq 0
.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
and we have type derivations
\Phi_{r}
and
\Phi_{u}
with
counters
\mathit{e}_{1}
and
\mathit{e}_{2}
resp. We conclude directly from the
i.h.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1
and we have type derivations
\Phi_{r}
and
\Phi_{u}
with counters

\mathit{e}_{1}
and
\mathit{e}_{2}
resp. both assigning multiset types. By
i.h.
we get

\mathit{e}_{1}>0
and
\mathit{e}_{2}>0
, and hence we conclude
\mathit{e}>0
as
expected.

•

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
,
\sigma\in\mathtt{tt}
,

\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1
and we have a derivation
\Phi_{r}
with
counter
\mathit{e}_{1}
and a multiset type, and another derivation
\Phi_{u}

with counter
\mathit{e}_{2}
and type
\mathtt{n}
. By
i.h.
we get
\mathit{e}_{1}>0

and
\mathit{e}_{2}\geq 0
, so we can safely conclude
\mathit{e}\geq 0
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t=\lambda{x}.{r}
,
\sigma=[{\mathcal{M}_{i}}\to{\tau_{i}}]_{i\in I}
,
\mathit{e}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}
for some set of indices
I
, and we have a type
derivation
\Phi^{i}_{r}
with counter
\mathit{e}_{i}
for each
i\in I
. By
i.h.
we get
\mathit{e}_{i}\geq 0
for every
i\in I
, and hence we conclude
\mathit{e}>0
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
and we have type derivations
\Phi_{r}
and
\Phi_{u}
with
counters
\mathit{e}_{1}
and
\mathit{e}_{2}
resp. We conclude directly from the
i.h.

∎

Soundness.

The soundness property is based on a series of auxiliary results that enables
to reason about tight type derivations:

Lemma 14

(Tight Spreading)
.

Let

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

such that
\Gamma
is tight.

1.

If
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0
, then
\sigma\in\mathtt{tt}
.

2.

If
\sigma=[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]
, then
t\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
.

Proof.

We show simultaneously the two properties by induction on
\Phi
.

1.

We consider every possible rule.

•

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
\sigma\in\mathtt{tt}
by definition.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
. The hypothesis implies
that the type derivation
\Phi_{r}
typing
r
with type
\sigma
has a
tight context, and the two first counters of
\Phi_{r}
are equal to
0
or

r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then the
i.h.
(
1
)
applied to
\Phi_{r}
gives
\sigma\in\mathtt{tt}
.

•

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t=x\notin\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
and
\mathit{e}>0
. Thus,
this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
. The
proof concludes with
\mathit{m}>0
so let us assume
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
, which
means in particular that
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, we
have a derivation

{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}
,
with
\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma
, so that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is also tight. The
i.h.
(
2
) gives
r\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
, so that

r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then we can apply the
i.h.
(
1
) to
\Phi_{r}
which gives

[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]\in\mathtt{tt}
, and this is a
contradiction. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. This case is similar to the previous one.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t=\lambda{x}.{u}\notin\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
.
Moreover, by Lem.
13
, we have
\mathit{e}>0
. Thus,
this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
. The hypothesis implies
that the type derivation
\Phi_{u}
typing
u
has a tight context, and the
two first counters of
\Phi_{u}
are equal to
0
or
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
.
Moreover, we know by construction that the type of
u
is a multiset. But
the
i.h.
(
1
) applied to
\Phi_{u}
gives a
tight type for
u
which leads to a contradiction.

2.

Let
\sigma=[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]
. Suppose
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
. We reason by cases.

•

t=x
. Then
\Phi
is necessarily
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
because of the
type
\sigma
, thus
\Gamma={x}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]}

contradicts the fact that
\Gamma
is tight.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
, with
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
and
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then
\Phi
necessarily ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
or

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. In the first case,
\Phi
contains a subderivation typing

u
with a context
\Delta\subseteq\Gamma
and a multiset type
\mathcal{N}
. Thus

\Delta
is also tight so that the
i.h.
(
1
) gives
\mathcal{N}\in\mathtt{tt}
which is a
contradiction because
\mathcal{N}
is a multiset. In the second case
\Phi

contains a subderivation typing
r
with a tight context and the same type

[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]
, so that the
i.h.
(
2
) gives
r\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
, which
implies
t\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
, contradiction.

∎

Lemma 15
.

Let

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

tight. Then,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0
iff
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
.

Proof.

\left.\Rightarrow\right)
We prove simultaneously the following statements by
induction on
\Phi
.

1.

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

tight and
\mathsf{var}(t)
implies
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
.

2.

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

tight and
\mathsf{app}(t)
implies
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
.

3.

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

tight and
\mathsf{abs}(t)
implies
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
.

•

\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
. Then
t=x
and
\mathsf{var}(t)
. By
definition
x\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
trivially holds.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
,
\sigma=\mathtt{n}
,

\Gamma={\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}+1
,

{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}}
, and

{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}
.
We are in the case
\mathsf{app}(t)
so we need to show
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Moreover,

\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
holds since
r
is typed with
\overline{\mathtt{vl}}

(
i.e.
\mathtt{vr}
or
\mathtt{n}
), and
\mathsf{abs}(r)
implies
\sigma
is

\mathtt{vl}
or a multiset type. By hypothesis
\Gamma^{\prime}
is tight, hence
i.h.
(
1
) or (
2
) gives
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
respectively. Similarly,
\Delta
is tight
and one of the
i.h.
gives
u\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
, since
\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}\subseteq\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
and
\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}\subseteq\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. We thus conclude
{r}\,{u}\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
t=\lambda{x}.{r}\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
by definition.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
,
\Gamma={({\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
,

{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}
,

{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}
and

\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))
. Moreover, note that
\mathsf{abs}(u)
implies its type is

\mathtt{vl}
or a multiset, while
\mathsf{var}(u)
implies its type is
\mathtt{vr}
,

\mathtt{vl}
or a multiset. Then,
\neg\mathsf{abs}(u)
and
\neg\mathsf{var}(u)
hold
since it is typed with
\mathtt{n}
. Since
{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}
is
tight and
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Delta
are both tight
as well. The
i.h.
(
2
) gives
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. On
the other hand, some of the
i.h.
applies and gives
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
r\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. We thus conclude
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
, respectively.

•

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. This case does not apply since it concludes with
\mathit{e}>0
.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
,
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. These cases do not apply since they
conclude with
\mathit{m}>0
.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. This case does not apply since, by
Lem.
13
, it concludes with
\mathit{e}>0
.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma={({\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
tight
such that, in particular,

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

with
\Delta
tight. By Lem.
14

(
1
) on
\Phi_{u}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)\in\mathtt{tt}
which leads to a contradiction since
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)
is a multiset
by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

\left.\Leftarrow\right)
By induction on
t
.

•

t=x
. Then,
\Phi
necessarily ends with rule
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
, or

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
and the statement trivially holds.

•

t={r}\,{u}
. By definition
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
gives
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
, and
u\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. There are three cases to
consider:

1.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
, then
\sigma=\mathtt{n}
,
\Gamma={\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}+1
,

{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}}
,

{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}
.
Moreover,
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Delta
are both tight. Then, the
i.h.
gives

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}=0
and
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=0
. Hence,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0
.

2.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, then
\Gamma={\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}+1
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}-1
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
and

{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]}}}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}
is tight. We know by Lem.
14

(
2
) that
r\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, it is
necessarily the case
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
and Lem.
14

(
1
) gives

[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]\in\mathtt{tt}
which is a contradiction.
Hence, this case does not apply.

3.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, this case is similar to the
previous one.

•

t=\lambda{x}.{r}
. There are two cases to consider for
\Phi
:

1.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
, then
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0

by definition.

2.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, then
\sigma
should be a
multiset of functional types, which contradicts the hypothesis of
\Phi

being tight. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
. By definition
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
implies
r\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
and
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}\subseteq\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, there are two cases to
consider for
\Phi
:

1.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
, then
\Gamma={({\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
,

{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}
,

{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}

and
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))
. Since
\Gamma
is tight and

\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Delta
are both tight as well.
Then, the
i.h.
with
r\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
and
u\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
gives
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}=0
and
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=0
. Hence,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0
.

2.

if
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
, then
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Gamma={({\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
tight such that, in
particular,

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

with
\Delta
tight. Moreover,
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
implies
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
.
By Lem.
14
(
1
)

\Gamma^{\prime}(x)\in\mathtt{tt}
which is a contradiction since
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)
is
a multiset type by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

∎

Lemma 16
.

Let

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}
.
If
\Phi
is tight, then
\mathit{s}=|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Phi
.

•

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
. Then
t=x
and
\mathit{s}=0=|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
,
\sigma=\mathtt{n}
,

\Gamma={\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}+1
,

{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}}
,

{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}
. Then,

\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Delta
are both tight, hence the
i.h.
gives
\mathit{s}^{\prime}=|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}
and
\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}=|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}
. Hence,
\mathit{s}=|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}+1=|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}
.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
t=\lambda{x}.{r}
, and
\mathit{s}=0=|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}
.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
,
\Gamma={({\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
,

{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}
,

{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}
and

\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))
. Since
\Gamma
is tight and
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))
,
then
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Delta
are both tight as well. Thus,
i.h.
gives
\mathit{s}^{\prime}=|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}
and
\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}=|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then,
\mathit{s}=|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}=|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}
.

•

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. This case does not apply since it concludes with
\mathit{e}>0
.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
,
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. These cases do not apply since they
conclude with
\mathit{m}>0
.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. This case does not apply since, by
Lem.
13
, it concludes with
\mathit{e}>0
.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
,
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma={({\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
tight
such that, in particular,

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

with
\Delta
tight. By Lem.
14

(
1
) on
\Phi_{u}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)\in\mathtt{tt}
which leads to a contradiction, since
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)
is a multiset
type by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

∎

A type derivation for a value with a multiset types might be split in
multiple derivations.

Lemma 17

(Split Type for Exact Value)
.

Let

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{v}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

such that
\mathcal{M}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\mathcal{M}_{i}}
. Then, there exist

({{\Phi_{v}^{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{i}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{v}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

such that
\Gamma={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{i}}
,
\mathit{m}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}
,
\mathit{e}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}-|I|
, and

\mathit{s}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the form of
v
.

•

v=x
. By
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}}
.
From
\mathcal{M}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\mathcal{M}_{i}}
we can build

({{\Phi^{i}_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

by using
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
several times, and conclude since

+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}=|I|
and
\mathit{e}=1
. Note that the persistent rules

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
and
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
do not apply since they do not conclude
with a multiset type.

•

v=\lambda{x}.{r}
. By
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, we have
\Gamma={\!}\mathrel{+_{j\in J}}{{\Gamma_{j}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}
,
\mathcal{M}=[{\Gamma_{j}(x)}\to{\tau_{j}}]_{j\in J}
,
\mathit{m}=+_{j\in J}{\mathit{m}_{j}}
,
\mathit{e}=1+_{j\in J}{\mathit{e}_{j}}
,
\mathit{s}=+_{j\in J}{\mathit{s}_{i}}
and

({{\Phi^{j}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{j}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{j}},{\mathit{e}_{j}},{\mathit{s}_{j}})}{{r}:{\tau_{j}}}}})_{j\in J}

for some
J
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{i}=[{\Gamma_{k}(x)}\to{\tau_{k}}]_{k\in K_{i}}
for each
i\in I
,
where
J=\biguplus_{i\in I}{K_{i}}
. Then, by using
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
we
build

({{\Phi^{i}_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{\!}\mathrel{+_{k\in K_{i}}}{{\Gamma_{k}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}}\vdash^{({+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{m}_{k}}},{1+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{e}_{k}}},{+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{s}_{k}}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

Finally, we conclude since:

–

\Gamma={\!}\mathrel{+_{j\in J}}{{\Gamma_{j}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{({\!}\mathrel{+_{k\in K_{i}}}{{\Gamma_{k}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}})}

–

\mathit{m}=+_{j\in J}{\mathit{m}_{j}}=+_{i\in I}{(+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{m}_{k}})}

–

\mathit{e}=1+_{j\in J}{\mathit{e}_{j}}=1+_{i\in I}{(1+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{e}_{k}})}-|I|

–

\mathit{s}=+_{j\in J}{\mathit{s}_{j}}=+_{i\in I}{(+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{s}_{k}})}

As before, the persistent rule
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
does not apply since it does
not conclude with a multiset type.

∎

These auxiliary lemmas are used in particular to show how to compute the
counters when substituting typed values. This is the key property to obtain
subject reduction.

Lemma 18

(Substitution)
.

Let

{\Phi_{t}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\tau}}}

and

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{v}:{\Gamma(x)}}}
.
Then,

{\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}-1},{\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}:{\tau}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.

•

t=x
. Then,
{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}=v
and there are three
possibilities for
\Phi_{t}
:

–

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
. Then
\tau=\mathtt{vr}
and
\Gamma(x)=[\mathtt{vr}]
. There is only one way of typing a value with

[\mathtt{vr}]
, namely
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, hence
v
is a variable and

\Delta={v}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}
. We conclude by taking

{\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{v}:{\mathtt{vr}}}}

by rule
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
. Note that the counters are as expected.

–

\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
. Then
\tau=\mathtt{vl}
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
.
There are only two ways of typing a value with type
[\,]
, namely

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, if
v
is a variable, and
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with
I=\emptyset
, if
v
is an abstraction. In both cases the counters of

\Phi_{v}
are
(0,1,0)
. We conclude by taking

{\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{v}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

by
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
, if
v
is a variable, or
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
, if
v
is an
abstraction.

–

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then we have

{\Phi_{t}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\tau}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\tau}}}
,

\tau
being a multiset type. We conclude by taking

\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}=\Phi_{v}
since the counters are as
expected.

•

t=y\neq x
. Then,
{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}=y
, and there are three
possibilities for
\Phi_{t}
:
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
and

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, with
\Gamma(x)=[\,]
in all cases. As observed above
the counters of
\Phi_{v}
are
(0,1,0)
whenever
\Gamma(x)=[\,]
, hence
we conclude by taking
\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}=\Phi_{t}
.

•

All the other cases are straightforward by the
i.h.
and
Lem.
17
.

∎

Lemma 19

(Exact Subject Reduction)
.

Let

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

be a tight derivation. If
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
, then there is

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{\sigma}}}

such that

(1)

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}-1
and

\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{m}
-step;

(2)

\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}-1
and

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{e}
-step.

Proof.

We actually prove the following stronger statement that allows us to reason
inductively:

Let
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
and

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

such that
\Gamma
is tight, and either
\sigma\in\mathtt{tt}
or

\neg\mathsf{abs}(t)
. Then, there exists

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{\sigma}}}

such that

1.

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}-1
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an

\mathtt{m}
-step.

2.

\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}-1
and
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an

\mathtt{e}
-step.

We proceed by induction on
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
.

•

t={{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{r}}\rangle}\,{u}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}\rangle=t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{m}
-step. We proceed
by induction on
\mathtt{L}
. We only show here the case
\mathtt{L}=\Box
as
the inductive case is straightforward.

We first remark that
\Phi
cannot end with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
since

\lambda{x}.{u}
cannot be typed neither with
\mathtt{vr}
nor with

\mathtt{n}
, then there are two cases depending on the last rule of

\Phi
.

1.

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
\Phi
has the following form:

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.94218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=143.0499pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{1+\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{[{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}\to{\sigma}]}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{r})}\,{u}}:{\sigma}}

with
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}

and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
with
the following type derivation

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}

\displaystyle{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}}

taking
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}=\mathit{m}-1
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}
.

2.

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
\Phi
has the following form:

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.94218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=143.0499pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{1+\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{[{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}\to{\sigma}]}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{r})}\,{u}}:{\sigma}}

with
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}

and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
with
the following type derivation

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}}

taking
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}=\mathit{m}-1
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}
.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{v}\rangle}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{{r}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}\rangle=t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{e}
-step. We
proceed by induction on
\mathtt{L}
. We only show here the case
\mathtt{L}=\Box
as the inductive case is straightforward.

Then,
t={r}[{x}\backslash{v}]
. Note that
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
does not apply since
it requires the value
v
to be typed with
\mathtt{n}
, which is not
possible. Thus, rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
is necessarily used and we have

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{v}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}

\displaystyle{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{v}]}:{\sigma}}}

with
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
and

\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. We conclude by applying
Lem.
18
with
\Phi_{r}
and
\Phi_{v}
, thus
obtaining

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}:{\sigma}}}
.
Note that the counters are as expected.

•

t={r}\,{u}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r^{\prime}}\,{u}=t^{\prime}
, where
r\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}r^{\prime}

and
\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
. Then,
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
,

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
or
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, and in either case the subterm
r
has an
associated typing derivation
\Phi_{r}
which verifies the hypothesis since

\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
. Hence, by
i.h.
there exists a
\Phi_{r^{\prime}}
with proper counters
such that we can derive
\Phi^{\prime}
applying the same rule as in
\Phi
to
conclude.

•

t={r}\,{u}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r}\,{u^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}
, where
u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}u^{\prime}

and
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}\cup\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
. There are two further cases to consider:

1.

If
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
, then the subterm
u
has an
associated typing derivation which verifies the hypothesis (context and
type are tight). Then the property holds by
i.h.

2.

If
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
or
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, then the
subterm
r
has an associated typing derivation with a tight context and a
functional type
[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]
.
Lem.
14
(
2
)
gives
r\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
so that
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then,
Lem.
14
(
1
)
gives
[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]\in\mathtt{tt}
which leads to a
contradiction. So this case is not possible.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r^{\prime}}[{x}\backslash{u}]=t^{\prime}
, where
r\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}r^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. There are two further cases to consider:

1.

If
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
then the subterm
r
has an
associated typing derivation which verifies the hypothesis (context tight
and
\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
). Then the property holds by
i.h.

2.

If
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
then the subterm
u
has an
associated typing derivation with a tight context and a multiset type
\mathcal{M}
.
Lem.
14
(
1
)
gives
\mathcal{M}\in\mathtt{tt}
which leads to a contradiction. So this case is
not possible.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r}[{x}\backslash{u^{\prime}}]=t^{\prime}
, where
u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}u^{\prime}
and
\neg\mathsf{val}(u)
. There are two further cases to consider:

1.

If
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
then the subterm
u
has an
associated typing derivation which verifies the hypothesis (context and
type are tight). Then the property holds by the
i.h.

2.

If
\Phi
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
then the subterm
u
has an
associated typing derivation
\Phi_{u}
which verifies the hypothesis since

\neg\mathsf{val}(u)
implies
\neg\mathsf{abs}(u)
. Hence, by
i.h.
there exists a

\Phi_{u^{\prime}}
with proper counters such that we can derive
\Phi^{\prime}
applying

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
as in
\Phi
to conclude.

∎

An interesting remark is that types of subterms in tight derivations may change
during CBV reduction, unlike other approaches
[
1
,
39
]
.
To illustrate this phenomenon, consider the following reduction

{x}[{x}\backslash{{\tt I}}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{\tt I}
. The terms on the left and right hand side
can be resp. tightly typed by the following derivations (we omit the counters):

{\mathtt{({val_{p}})}}

\displaystyle{{}\vdash{{x}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

{\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}}

\displaystyle{{}\vdash{{{\tt I}}:{[\,]}}}

{\mathtt{({es_{c}})}}

\displaystyle{{}\vdash{{{x}[{x}\backslash{{\tt I}}]}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

{\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}}

\displaystyle{{}\vdash{{{\tt I}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

Notice that the identity function
{\tt I}
is typed differently in each
derivation: the substitution introduced by rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
is a consuming
constructor, which disappears when
\mathtt{sv}
-reduction consumes its value
argument, a phenomenon that is captured by means of a multitype for the
argument of the substitution. Indeed,
{\tt I}
on the left-hand side derivation is
typed with the multitype
[\,]
. This value
{\tt I}
substitutes a variable
x

typed with
\mathtt{vl}
, which is just a placeholder for a persistent value.
Thus, once the substitution is performed, the identity
{\tt I}
becomes persistent
on the right-hand side, a phenomenon which is naturally captured by the tight
type
\mathtt{vl}
. This observation also applies to the tight typing system of
CBPV in
Sec.
6
.

Theorem 20

(Soundness)
.

If

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

is tight, then there exists
p
such that
p\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
and

t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps,

\mathit{e}

\mathtt{e}
-steps, and
|{p}|_{\mathtt{v}}=\mathit{s}
.

Proof.

We prove the statement for
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}
and then conclude for the general
notion of reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}
by the observation that all
reduction sequences to normal form have the same number of multiplicative and
exponential steps. Let

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}
:

•

If
\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}=0
, then
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0
and
Lem.
15
gives
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Moreover, by
Lem.
16
we get
|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}=\mathit{s}
. Thus, we
conclude with
p=t
.

•

If
\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}>0
, then
t\notin\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
holds by
Lem.
15
and thus there exists
t^{\prime}
such that
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(1,0)}t^{\prime}
or
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(0,1)}t^{\prime}
by
Prop.
1
. By
Lem.
19
there exists a type derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{\sigma}}}

such that
1+\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}
. By the
i.h.
there exists
p\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
such that
t^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m}^{\prime},\mathit{e}^{\prime})}p
with
\mathit{s}=|{p}|_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(1,0)}t^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m}^{\prime},\mathit{e}^{\prime})}p
or

t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(0,1)}t^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m}^{\prime},\mathit{e}^{\prime})}p
which means
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
, as expected.

∎

As in CBN, the previous theorem is stated by using the general notion of
reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}
, but the proofs (notably
Lem.
19
) are done using the deterministic
reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}
. Similar comment applies to the forthcoming
Thm.
25
.

Completeness.

The completeness result is also based on intermediate lemmas, most notably the
so-called
tight typing of normal forms
and
subject expansion

properties:

Lemma 21

(Tight Typing of Normal Forms)
.

If
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
, then there is a tight derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}
.

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on the following claims:

1.

If
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
, then there
exists a tight derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{vr}}}}
.

2.

If
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
, then there
exists a tight derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}
.

3.

If
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
, then there
exists a tight derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}}
.

4.

If
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
, then there
exists a tight derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{tt}}}}
.

•

t=x
. Then,
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
and we conclude
(
1
) by
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
or
(
2
) by
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
, since
|{x}|_{\mathtt{v}}=0
by definition.

•

t={r}\,{u}
. Then,
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
with
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}\cup\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
and
u\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. By
i.h.
(
4
) on

u
we have

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{tt}}}}

with
\Delta
tight. There are two options for
r
, namely
r\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}

or
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. By
i.h.
(
1
) or
(
3
) resp. there exists a tight derivation

{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{r}:{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}}}
.
We conclude (
3
) by
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
with

\Phi_{r}
and
\Phi_{u}
, obtaining
\Gamma={\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}

and
|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}=|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}+1
as expected.

•

t=\lambda{x}.{r}
. Then, we conclude
(
4
) by
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
since
|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}=0
by definition.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
. Then, there are three cases to consider:
t\in\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
,
t\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
or
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. In either of them we have

u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Thus, by
i.h.
(
3
) there
exists a tight derivation

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}
.
Moreover, in each case we get
r
in the same set as
t
by definition. By
applying the respective
i.h.
on
r
we get a tight derivation

{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}

with the appropiate type for each case. Finally, since
\Gamma^{\prime}
tight
implies
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)
tight, we conclude each respective claim by

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
with
\Gamma={{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}

tight and, hence,

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({0},{0},{|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}}

tight as well. Note that
|{t}|_{\mathtt{v}}=|{r}|_{\mathtt{v}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{v}}
.

∎

Analogously to the soundness case, we may obtain a single type
derivation for a value with multiset type from several ones, properly combining
their counters, and hence leading to an anti-subtitution result.

Lemma 22

(Merge type for value)
.

Let

({{\Phi_{v}^{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{i}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{v}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}
.
Then, there exists

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{v}:{\mathcal{M}}}}
.
such that
\Gamma={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{i}}
,
\mathcal{M}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\mathcal{M}_{i}}
,
\mathit{m}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}
,
\mathit{e}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}-|I|
, and
\mathit{s}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the form of
v
.

•

v=x
. By
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, we have

({{\Phi^{i}_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\mathcal{M}_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}
.
Then, we conclude by
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}}
,
where
\mathcal{M}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\mathcal{M}_{i}}
and hence
\Gamma={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{i}}
as expected. Moreover,

+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}=0
and

+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}=|I|
, hence
\mathit{e}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}-|I|
.
Note that the persistent rules
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
and
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
do not
apply since they do not conclude with a multiset type.

•

v=\lambda{x}.{r}
. By
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, for each
i\in I
we have

\Gamma_{i}={\!}\mathrel{+_{k\in K_{i}}}{{\Gamma_{k}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{i}=[{\Gamma_{k}(x)}\to{\tau_{k}}]_{k\in K_{i}}
with
\mathit{m}_{i}=+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{m}_{k}}
,
\mathit{e}_{i}=1+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{e}_{k}}
,
\mathit{s}_{i}=+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{s}_{k}}
and type derivations

({{\Phi^{k}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{k}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{k}},{\mathit{e}_{k}},{\mathit{s}_{k}})}{{r}:{\tau_{k}}}}})_{k\in K_{i}}

for each
i\in I
. Let
J=\biguplus_{i\in I}{K_{i}}
and
\mathcal{M}=[{\Gamma_{j}(x)}\to{\tau_{j}}]_{j\in J}=[{\Gamma_{k}(x)}\to{\tau_{k}}]_{k\in+_{i\in I}{K_{i}}}
We
conclude since, by
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, we can build the derivation

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({+_{j\in J}{\mathit{m}_{j}}},{1+_{j\in J}{\mathit{e}_{j}}},{+_{j\in J}{\mathit{s}_{j}}})}{{v}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

where:

–

\Gamma={\!}\mathrel{+_{j\in J}}{{\Gamma_{j}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{({\!}\mathrel{+_{k\in K_{i}}}{{\Gamma_{k}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}})}={\!}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{i}}

–

\mathit{m}=+_{j\in J}{\mathit{m}_{j}}=+_{i\in I}{(+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{m}_{k}})}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}

–

\mathit{e}=1+_{j\in J}{\mathit{e}_{j}}=1+_{i\in I}{(1+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{e}_{k}})}-|I|=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}-|I|

–

\mathit{s}=+_{j\in J}{\mathit{s}_{j}}=+_{i\in I}{(+_{k\in K_{i}}{\mathit{s}_{k}})}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}

As before, the persistent rule
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
does not apply since it does
not conclude with a multiset type.

∎

Dually to the substitution lemma, the following property states how to extract
type derivations (and thus counters) from substituted term.

Lemma 23

(Anti-Substitution)
.

Let

{\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}:{\tau}}}
.
Then, there exist

{\Phi_{t}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\tau}}}

and

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{v}:{\Gamma(x)}}}

such that
\Gamma^{\prime}={{\Gamma}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}
,
\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}-1
and
\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.

•

t=x
. Then,
{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}=v
and we analyze the shape of

\tau
:

–

\tau\in\mathtt{tt}
. Then, there are three further possibilities:

*

\tau=\mathtt{n}
. This case does not apply since there is no
way to derive type
\mathtt{n}
for a value.

*

\tau=\mathtt{vl}
. Then,
\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}
can only
be
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
or
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Thus,
\Gamma^{\prime}
is empty and

\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}=0
. We conclude with

{\Phi_{t}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

by
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
, and

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{v}:{[\,]}}}

by either
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
or
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with
I=\emptyset
.

*

\tau=\mathtt{vr}
. Then,
v
is necessarily a variable
z
,
since abstractions cannot be assigned
\mathtt{vr}
, and

\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}
can only be
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
. Thus,
\Gamma^{\prime}={z}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}
and
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}=0
. We conclude with

{\Phi_{t}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{vr}}}}

by
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
and

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{z}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{z}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}}

by
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
.

–

\tau=\mathcal{M}
. Then, we take
\Phi_{v}=\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}

and

{\Phi_{t}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\tau}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\tau}}}

by
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
to conclude. Note that the counters are as expected.

–

\tau={\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}
. This case does not apply since there
is no way to derive an arrow type for a value.

•

t=y\neq x
. Then,
{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}=y
and

\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}
is either

{{y}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{y}:{\mathtt{vr}}}

by
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
, or

{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{y}:{\mathtt{vl}}}
by
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
, or

{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{y}:{[\,]}}
by
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. In either case we
have
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)=[\,]
, thus we take
\Phi_{t}=\Phi_{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}
and conclude with

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{v}:{[\,]}}}
by
either
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
or
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with
I=\emptyset
. Note that the
counters are as expected.

•

All the other cases are straightforward by the
i.h.
and
Lem.
22
.

∎

Lemma 24

(Exact Subject Expansion)
.

Let

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{\sigma}}}

be a tight derivation. If
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
, then there is

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

such that

(1)

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}-1
and

\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{m}
-step;

(2)

\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}-1
and

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{e}
-step.

Proof.

We actually prove the following stronger statement that allows us to reason
inductively:

Let
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
and

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{\sigma}}}

such that
\Gamma
is tight, and either
\sigma\in\mathtt{tt}
or

\neg\mathsf{abs}(t)
. Then, there exists

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

such that

1.

\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}-1
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an

\mathtt{m}
-step.

2.

\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}-1
and
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
is an

\mathtt{e}
-step.

We proceed by induction on
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}t^{\prime}
.

•

t={{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{r}}\rangle}\,{u}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}\rangle=t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{m}
-step. We
proceed by induction on
\mathtt{L}
. We only show here the case
\mathtt{L}=\Box
as the inductive case is straightforward.

Then,
t^{\prime}={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
and there are two possibilities for
\Phi^{\prime}
.

1.

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
\Phi^{\prime}
has the following form:

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}

with
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}
,
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}

and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
and

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with the following type derivation

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.94218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=143.0499pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{1+\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{[{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}\to{\sigma}]}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma^{\prime}(x))

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{r})}\,{u}}:{\sigma}}}

taking
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+1
and
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}
.

2.

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. Then
\Phi^{\prime}
has the following form:

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}

with
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}
,
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}

and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
and

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with the following type derivation

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.94218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=143.0499pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{1+\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{[{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}\to{\sigma}]}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{r})}\,{u}}:{\sigma}}}

taking
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+1
and
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}
.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{v}\rangle}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{{r}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}}\rangle=t^{\prime}
is an
\mathtt{e}
-step. We
proceed by induction on
\mathtt{L}
. We only show here the case
\mathtt{L}=\Box
as the inductive case is straightforward.

Then,
t^{\prime}={{r}\left\{{x}\backslash{v}\right\}}
. Lem.
23

with
\Phi^{\prime}
, there exist type derivations

{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}

and

{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{v}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

such that
\Gamma={{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}
,
\mathit{m}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}
,
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1
and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
with the following type
derivation

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{v}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{v}:{\Gamma^{\prime}(x)}}}

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}

\displaystyle{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{{\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{v}]}:{\sigma}}}

taking
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}=\mathit{m}^{\prime}
and
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}+1
.

•

t={r}\,{u}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r^{\prime}}\,{u}=t^{\prime}
, where
r\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}r^{\prime}

and
\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
. Then,
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
,

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
or
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, and in either case the subterm
r^{\prime}
has an
associated typing derivation
\Phi_{r^{\prime}}
which verifies the hypothesis since

\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
. Hence, by
i.h.
there exists a
\Phi_{r}
with proper counters
such that we can derive
\Phi
applying the same rule as in
\Phi^{\prime}
to
conclude.

•

t={r}\,{u}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r}\,{u^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}
, where
u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}u^{\prime}

and
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}\cup\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
. There are two further cases to consider:

1.

If
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
, then the subterm
u^{\prime}
has
an associated typing derivation which verifies the hypothesis (context and
type are tight). Then the property holds by
i.h.

2.

If
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
or
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
, then the
subterm
r
has an associated typing derivation with a tight context and a
functional type
[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]
.
Lem.
14
(
2
)
gives
r\notin\mathsf{vr}_{\mathtt{v}}
so that
r\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Then,
Lem.
14
(
1
)
gives
[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]\in\mathtt{tt}
which leads to a
contradiction. So this case is not possible.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r^{\prime}}[{x}\backslash{u}]=t^{\prime}
, where
r\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}r^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathsf{ne}_{\mathtt{v}}
. There are two further cases to consider:

1.

If
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
then the subterm
r^{\prime}
has an
associated typing derivation which verifies the hypothesis (context tight
and
\neg\mathsf{abs}(r)
). Then the property holds by
i.h.

2.

If
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
then the subterm
u
has an
associated typing derivation with a tight context and a multiset type
\mathcal{M}
.
Lem.
14
(
1
)
gives
\mathcal{M}\in\mathtt{tt}
which leads to a contradiction. So this case is
not possible.

•

t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}{r}[{x}\backslash{u^{\prime}}]=t^{\prime}
, where
u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}u^{\prime}
and
\neg\mathsf{val}(u)
. There are two further cases to consider:

1.

If
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
then the subterm
u^{\prime}
has
an associated typing derivation which verifies the hypothesis (context and
type are tight). Then the property holds by the
i.h.
.

2.

If
\Phi^{\prime}
ends with rule
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
then the subterm

u^{\prime}
has an associated typing derivation
\Phi_{u^{\prime}}
which verifies the
hypothesis since
\neg\mathsf{val}(u)
implies
\neg\mathsf{abs}(u)
. Hence, by
i.h.
there
exists a
\Phi_{u}
with proper counters such that we can derive
\Phi

applying
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
as in
\Phi^{\prime}
to conclude.

∎

Notice that tight derivations properly counts, separately,
\mathtt{m}
-steps and

\mathtt{e}
-steps. As a consequence, completeness follows.

Theorem 25

(Completeness)
.

If
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
p\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
, then
there exists a tight type derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{|{p}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}
.

Proof.

As for soundness (Thm.
20
) prove the
statement for
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}
and then conclude for the general notion of reduction

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}
. Let
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
. We proceed by
induction on
\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}
:

•

If
\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}=0
, then
\mathit{m}=\mathit{e}=0
and thus
t=p
,
which implies
t\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
. Lem.
21
allows
to conclude.

•

If
\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}>0
, then there is
t^{\prime}
such that
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(1,0)}t^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m}-1,\mathit{e})}p
or
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(0,1)}t^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{dv}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e}-1)}p
. By the
i.h.
there is a tight derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{|{p}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{\sigma}}}

such
\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}-1
.
Lem.
24
gives a tight derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}},{|{p}|_{\mathtt{v}}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

such
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{m}^{\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}+1
. We then have
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}+\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{m}+\mathit{e}
. The fact that
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{m}
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}=\mathit{e}
holds by a simple case analysis.

∎

Example 26
.

Consider
t_{0}={{{\tt K}}\,{({z}\,{{\tt I}})}}\,{({{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}})}
from
Ex.
12
but now in the CBV setting. It

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}
-reduces in 3
\mathtt{m}
-steps and 2
\mathtt{e}
-steps to

{x}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
, whose
\mathtt{v}
-size is 1,
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lllllllll}t_{0}&=&{\underline{{{\tt K}}\,{({z}\,{{\tt I}})}}}\,{({{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}})}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&\underline{{{(\lambda{y}.{x})}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]}\,{({{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}})}}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&{{x}[{y}\backslash{\underline{{{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}}}}]}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]\\
&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&{\underline{{x}[{y}\backslash{{w}[{w}\backslash{{\tt I}}]}]}}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{sv}}&{\underline{{x}[{w}\backslash{{\tt I}}]}}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{sv}}&{x}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]\end{array}

System
\mathcal{V}
admits a proper tight type derivation for
t_{0}={{{\tt K}}\,{({z}\,{{\tt I}})}}\,{({{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}})}
from
Ex.
26
, with the expected final counter
(3,2,1)
, as shown in
Fig.
5
.

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{vr}}}

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\lambda{y}.{x}}:{[{[\,]}\to{\mathtt{vr}}]}}

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{2},{0})}{{{\tt K}}:{[{[\mathtt{vr}]}\to{[{[\,]}\to{\mathtt{vr}}]}]}}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{z}:{\mathtt{vr}}}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{{\tt I}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({1},{1},{1})}{{{{\tt K}}\,{({z}\,{{\tt I}})}}:{[{[\,]}\to{\mathtt{vr}}]}}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{w}:{[\,]}}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{2},{0})}{{{\tt I}}:{[{[\,]}\to{[\,]}]}}
