.

5
The
\lambda!
-Calculus

This section briefly presents the
bang calculus at a
distance

[
12
]
, called
\lambda!
-calculus. It is a
(conservative) extension of the original bang
calculus
[
25
,
26
]
, it uses ES operators and

reduction at a distance

[
5
]
, thus integrating
commutative conversions without jeopardising confluence
(see
[
12
]
for a discussion). Indeed,
T. Ehrhard
[
25
]
studies the CBPV from a Linear Logic (LL) point of
view by extending the
\lambda
-calculus with two new unary constructors

bang
(
\mathop{!}{\!}
) and
dereliction
(
\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{\!}
), playing
the role of the CBPV primitives
\mathtt{thunk}
/
\mathtt{force}
respectively.
His calculus suffers from the absence of
commutative
conversions

[
44
,
15
]
, making some redexes to be
syntactically blocked when open terms are considered. As a consequence, some
normal forms are semantically equivalent to non-terminating programs, a
situation which is clearly unsound. The bang calculus
[
26
]
adds
commutative conversions specified by means of
\sigma
-reduction rules, which
are crucial to unveil hidden (blocked) redexes. This approach, however,
presents a major drawback since the resulting combined reduction relation is
not confluent. The
\lambda!
-calculus
[
12
]
fixes these two
problems at the same time. Indeed, the syntax of the bang calculus is enriched
with explicit substitutions (ES), and
\sigma
-equivalence is integrated in
the primary reduction system by using the
distance

paradigm
[
5
]
, without any need to unveil hidden redexes by
means of an independent relation.

We consider the following grammar for terms (denoted by
\mathcal{T}
) and
contexts:

(Terms)

t,u

::=

x\in\mathcal{X}\mid{t}\,{u}\mid\lambda{x}.{t}\mid\mathop{!}{t}\mid\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{t}\mid{t}[{x}\backslash{u}]

(List contexts)

\mathtt{L}

::=

\Box\mid{\mathtt{L}}[{x}\backslash{t}]

(Surface contexts)

\mathtt{S}

::=

\Box\mid{\mathtt{S}}\,{t}\mid{t}\,{\mathtt{S}}\mid\lambda{x}.{\mathtt{S}}\mid\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{\mathtt{S}}\mid{\mathtt{S}}[{x}\backslash{u}]\mid{t}[{x}\backslash{\mathtt{S}}]

Special terms are
\Delta_{\mathop{!}{}}=\lambda{x}.{{x}\,{\mathop{!}{x}}}
, and
\Omega_{\mathop{!}{}}={\Delta_{\mathop{!}{}}}\,{\mathop{!}{\Delta_{\mathop{!}{}}}}
. Surface
contexts do not allow the symbol
\Box
to occur inside the bang constructor

\mathop{!}{}
. This is similar to
weak
contexts in
\lambda
-calculus,
where
\Box
cannot occur inside
\lambda
-abstractions. As we will see in
Sec.
7
, surface reduction in the
\lambda!
-calculus
is perfectly sufficient to capture head reduction in CBN, disallowing reduction
inside arguments, as well as open CBV, disallowing reduction inside
abstractions. Finally, we define the
\mathtt{f}
-size
of terms as
follows:

\begin{array}[]{lll@{\hspace{1cm}}lll@{\hspace{1cm}}lll}|{x}|_{\mathtt{f}}&:=&0\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&|{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{t}}|_{\mathtt{f}}&:=&|{t}|_{\mathtt{f}}\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&|{{t}[{x}\backslash{u}]}|_{\mathtt{f}}&:=&|{t}|_{\mathtt{f}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{f}}\\
|{\mathop{!}{t}}|_{\mathtt{f}}&:=&0\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&|{\lambda{x}.{t}}|_{\mathtt{f}}&:=&1+|{t}|_{\mathtt{f}}\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&|{{t}\,{u}}|_{\mathtt{f}}&:=&1+|{t}|_{\mathtt{f}}+|{u}|_{\mathtt{f}}\end{array}

The
\lambda!
-calculus
is given by the set of terms
\mathcal{T}

and the
(surface) reduction relation

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
, which is
defined as the
union
of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}
,
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{s!}}

(
s
ubstitute bang) and
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{d!}}
(
d
istant bang),
defined respectively as the closure by contexts
\mathtt{S}
of the following
three rewriting rules:

\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{t}}\rangle}\,{u}&\mathrel{\mapsto_{\mathtt{dB}}}&{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{t}[{x}\backslash{u}]}\rangle\\
{t}[{x}\backslash{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{u}}\rangle}]&\mathrel{\mapsto_{\mathtt{s!}}}&{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{u}\right\}}}\rangle\\
\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{t}}\rangle)}&\mathrel{\mapsto_{\mathtt{d!}}}&{\mathtt{L}}\langle{t}\rangle\end{array}

The rules are defined
at a distance
, as in CBN/CBV, in the sense that
the list context
\mathtt{L}
allows the main constructors involved in the
rules to be separated by an arbitrary finite list of substitutions. This new
formulation integrates commutative conversions inside the main (logical)
reduction rules of the calculus, thus inheriting the benefits enumerated in
Sec.
1
. Indeed, rule
\mathtt{s!}
can be decomposed in two different
rules
{t}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{u}}]\mathrel{\mapsto}{{t}\left\{{x}\backslash{u}\right\}}
and

{t}[{x}\backslash{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{u}}\rangle}]\mathrel{\mapsto}{\mathtt{L}}\langle{{t}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{u}}]}\rangle
, while
\mathtt{d!}
can be
decomposed in
\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(\mathop{!}{t})}\mathrel{\mapsto}t
and

\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{t}}\rangle)}\mathrel{\mapsto}{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{\mathop{!}{t}}}\rangle
. We write
\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}

for the reflexive-transitive closure of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
. Given the
translation of the bang calculus into LL proof-nets
[
25
]
, we
refer to
\mathtt{dB}
-steps as
\mathtt{m}
ultiplicative
and

\mathtt{s!}
/
\mathtt{d!}
-steps as
\mathtt{e}
xponential
steps. We write
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}u
if
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}u
using

\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps and
\mathit{e}

\mathtt{e}
-steps.

Example 27
.

Consider the following reduction sequence from
t^{\prime}_{0}={{{\tt K}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}
:

\begin{array}[]{lllll}t^{\prime}_{0}={\underline{{{\tt K}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&\underline{{{(\lambda{y}.{x})}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}]}\,{(\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&\underline{{{x}[{y}\backslash{\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}]}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}]}\\
&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{s!}}&\underline{{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}[{y}\backslash{\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}]}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{s!}}&{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}\end{array}

Notice that the second
\mathtt{dB}
-step uses action at a distance, where

\mathtt{L}
is
{\Box}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}]
.

The relation
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
enjoys a weak diamond property,

i.e.
one-step divergence can be closed in one step if the diverging terms are
different. This property has two important consequences.

Theorem 28

(Confluence
[
12
]
)
.

The reduction relation
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
is confluent.
Moreover, any two different
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
-reduction paths to normal form have the same length.

The second point relies essentially on the fact that reductions are
disallowed under bangs. An important consequence is that we can focus on any
particular
deterministic
strategy for the
\lambda!
-calculus, without
changing the number of steps to
\mathtt{f}
-normal form.

Neutral, Normal, and Clash-Free Terms.

A term is said to be
\mathtt{f}
-normal
if there is no
t^{\prime}
such that

t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}t^{\prime}
, in which case we write
t\not\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
.
However, some ill-formed
\mathtt{f}
-normal terms are not still the ones that
represent a desired result for a computation, they are called
clashes

(meta-variable
c
), and take one of the following forms:

{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{t}}\rangle}\,{u}
,

{t}[{y}\backslash{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{u}}\rangle}]
,

\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{u}}\rangle)}
, or

{t}\,{({{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{u}}\rangle})}
. Remark that in the three
first kind of clashes, replacing
\lambda{x}.{\!}
by
\mathop{!}{\!}
, and
inversely, creates a (root) redex, namely

{({\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{t}}\rangle)}\,{u}
,

{t}[{x}\backslash{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{t}}\rangle}]
and

\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{t}}\rangle)}
, respectively.

A term is
clash free
if it does not reduce to a term containing a
clash, it is
surface clash free
, written
{\tt scf}
, if it does not
reduce to a term containing a clash outside the scope of any constructor
!
.
Thus,
t
is not
{\tt scf}
if and only if there exist a surface context

\mathtt{S}
and a clash
c
such that
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}{\mathtt{S}}\langle{c}\rangle
. Surface clash free normal terms can be characterised
as follows:

(Neutral
{\tt scf}
)

\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}

::=

x\in\mathcal{X}\mid{\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}}\,{\mathsf{na}_{{\tt scf}}}\mid\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}})}\mid{\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}}[{x}\backslash{\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}}]

(Neutral-Abs
{\tt scf}
)

\mathsf{na}_{{\tt scf}}

::=

\mathop{!}{t}\mid\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}\mid{\mathsf{na}_{{\tt scf}}}[{x}\backslash{\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}}]

(Neutral-Bang
{\tt scf}
)

\mathsf{nb}_{{\tt scf}}

::=

\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}\mid\lambda{x}.{\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}}\mid{\mathsf{nb}_{{\tt scf}}}[{x}\backslash{\mathsf{ne}_{{\tt scf}}}]

(Normal
{\tt scf}
)

\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}

::=

\mathsf{na}_{{\tt scf}}\mid\mathsf{nb}_{{\tt scf}}

Proposition 29

(Clash-Free Normal Terms
[
12
]
)
.

Let
t\in\mathcal{T}
. Then
t
is a surface clash free
\mathtt{f}
-normal
term iff
{t}\in\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}
.

6
A Tight Type System for the
\lambda!
-Calculus

The methodology used to define the type system
\mathcal{B}
for the

\lambda!
-calculus is based on
[
12
]
, inspired in turn
from
[
19
,
9
,
1
]
, which defines
non-idempotent intersection type systems to count reduction lengths for
different evaluation strategies in the
\lambda
-calculus. In the case of the

\lambda!
-calculus, however, Thm.
28

guarantees that all reduction paths to normal form have the same length, so
that it is not necessary to reason w.r.t. any particular evaluation strategy.

The grammar of types of system
\mathcal{B}
is given by:

(Tight Types)

\mathtt{tt}

::=

\mathtt{n}\mid\mathtt{a}\mid\mathtt{vl}

(Types)

\sigma,\tau

::=

\mathtt{tt}\mid\mathcal{M}\mid{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}

(Multitype)

\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}

::=

[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}
where
I
is a finite set

The constant
\mathtt{a}
(resp.
\mathtt{vl}
) types terms whose normal form has
the shape
{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{t}}\rangle
(resp.

{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{t}}\rangle
,
i.e.
values
in the

\lambda!
-calculus sense), and the constant
\mathtt{n}
types terms whose
normal form is a neutral
{\tt scf}
. As before, the notions of
tightness

for multitypes, contexts and derivations are exactly the same used for CBN.
Typing rules are split in two groups: the
persistent
rules
(Fig.
6
) and the
consuming
ones
(Fig.
7
).

\kern-20.00003pt\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.40973pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}\quad{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{a}}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=123.23688pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({app_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}+1})}{{{t}\,{u}}:{\mathtt{n}}}$}}}}\qquad{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{tt}}}\quad\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma(x))$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=111.72661pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 8.9687pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}+1})}{{\lambda{x}.{t}}:{\mathtt{a}}}$}}}}\\
\\
{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{t}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=49.01393pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{t}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}$}}}}\quad{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.95834pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=63.09464pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{t}}:{\mathtt{n}}}$}}}}\quad{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\tau}}\kern 5.0pt{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}\kern 5.0pt\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma(x))$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=157.08762pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({es_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 1.15619pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{({\Gamma}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{t}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\tau}}$}}}}\end{array}

Figure 6:
System
\mathcal{B}
for the
\lambda!
-Calculus: Persistent Typing Rules.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.15167pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{t}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=63.31728pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({var_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{x}:{[\sigma]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\sigma}}$}}}}\qquad{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}}}\quad{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{u}:{\mathcal{M}}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=137.87238pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({app_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 7.75696pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}+1},{\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{t}\,{u}}:{\tau}}$}}}}\\
\\
{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}}}\quad{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}\quad\mathsf{tight}(\mathcal{M})$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=180.67795pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({appt_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 29.15974pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}+1},{\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{t}\,{u}}:{\tau}}$}}}}\\
\\
{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 29.61465pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\tau}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=107.52884pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{\lambda{x}.{t}}:{{\Gamma(x)}\to{\tau}}}$}}}}\qquad{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.88258pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty({\Gamma_{i}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{t}:{\sigma_{i}}})_{i\in I}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=164.1536pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{i}}}\vdash^{({+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}},{1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}},{+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}})}{{\mathop{!}{t}}:{[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}}}$}}}}\\
\\
{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 4.18056pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{[\sigma]}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=63.5588pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{t}}:{\sigma}}$}}}}\qquad{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 10.24658pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}\quad{\Delta}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{u}:{\Gamma(x)}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=156.11783pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({es_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{({\Gamma}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+}{\Delta}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}+\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}+\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{t}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}$}}}}\end{array}

Figure 7:
System
\mathcal{B}
for the
\lambda!
-Calculus: Consuming Typing Rules.

As in CBV, the
\mathtt{s!}
exponential steps do not only depend on a (consuming)
substitution constructor, but on the (bang) form of its argument. This makes
the exponential counting more subtle. Notice also that rule
\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}
does
not count
\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{\!}
constructors, according to the definition of

|{\_}|_{\mathtt{f}}
given in Sec.
5
and in contrast
to
[
12
]
. This is to keep a more intuitive relation with the
CBN/CBV translations (Sec.
2
), where
\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{\!}
plays
a silent role.

As in
[
12
]
, system
\mathcal{B}
is quantitatively sound and
complete. More precisely,

Theorem 30

(Soundness and Completeness)
.

1.

If

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

is tight, then there exists
p
such that
{p}\in\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}
and

t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps,

\mathit{e}

\mathtt{e}
-steps, and
|{p}|_{\mathtt{f}}=\mathit{s}
.

2.

If

t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
{p}\in\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}
, then there
exists a tight type derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{|{p}|_{\mathtt{f}}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}
.

Proof.

Similar to
[
12
]
.
∎

Example 31
.

Consider
t^{\prime}_{0}={{{\tt K}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}

from Ex.
27
, which normalises in
2

\mathtt{m}
-steps and
2

\mathtt{e}
-steps to
{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}\in\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}
of

\mathtt{f}
-size
1
. A tight derivation for
t^{\prime}_{0}
with appropriate
final counters
(2,2,1)
is given below.

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\lambda{y}.{x}}:{{[\,]}\to{\mathtt{n}}}}

\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{{\tt K}}:{{[\mathtt{n}]}\to{{[\,]}\to{\mathtt{n}}}}}

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{z}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{1})}{{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{1})}{{\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}

\mathtt{({app_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({1},{1},{1})}{{{{\tt K}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}:{{[\,]}\to{\mathtt{n}}}}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})}}:{[\,]}}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({2},{2},{1})}{{{{{\tt K}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

Notice that the only persistent rules are
\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}
and

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}
, used to type
{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}
. Indeed,

{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}
is the
\mathtt{f}
-normal form of
t^{\prime}_{0}
.

7
Untyped Translations

CBN/CBV (untyped) encodings into the bang
calculus
[
32
]
, inspired from Girard’s encodings, establish two
translations
cbn
and
cbv
, such that when
t
reduces to
u
in CBN (resp.
CBV),
cbn(t)
reduces to
cbn(u)
(resp.
cbv(t)
reduces to
cbv(u)
) in the
bang calculus.
These two encodings are dual: CBN forbids reduction inside arguments, which are
translated to bang terms, while CBV forbids reduction under

\lambda
-abstractions, also translated to bang terms.

In this paper we use alternative encodings. For CBN, we slightly adapt to
explicit substitutions Girard’s translation into LL
[
29
]
. The
resulting encoding preserves normal forms and is sound and complete with
respect to the standard (quantitative) type system in
[
28
]
. For
CBV, we discard the original encoding in
[
32
]
for two reasons:
CBV normal forms are not necessarily translated to normal forms in the bang
calculus (see
[
32
]
), and levels of terms (the level of
t
is
the number of
\mathop{!}{\!}
surrounding
t
) are not preserved either
(see
[
27
]
). We thus adopt the CBV encoding
in
[
12
]
which preserves normal forms as well as levels.

The CBN and CBV embedding into the
\lambda!
-calculus, written
{\_}^{\mathtt{n}}
and

{\_}^{\mathtt{v}}
resp., are inductively defined as:

\begin{array}[]{c@{\qquad}c}\begin{array}[]{rcl}{x}^{\mathtt{n}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&x\\
{(\lambda{x}.{t})}^{\mathtt{n}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\lambda{x}.{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}\\
{({t}\,{u})}^{\mathtt{n}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}\,{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\\
{({t}[{x}\backslash{u}])}^{\mathtt{n}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}]\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{rcl}{x}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\mathop{!}{x}\\
{(\lambda{x}.{t})}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\mathop{!}{\lambda{x}.{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}}\\
{({t}\,{u})}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{{\mathtt{L}}\langle{s}\rangle}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}&\text{if ${t}^{\mathtt{v}}={\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{s}}\rangle$}\\
{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({t}^{\mathtt{v}})}}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\\
{({t}[{x}\backslash{u}])}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}[{x}\backslash{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}]\end{array}\end{array}

Both translations extend to list contexts
\mathtt{L}
as expected. Remark that
there are no two consecutive
\mathop{!}{\!}
constructors in the image of the
translation. The CBN embedding extends Girard’s translation to ES, while the
CBV one is different. Indeed, the translation of an application

{t}\,{u}
is usually defined as
{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({t}^{\mathtt{v}})}}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}

(see
e.g.
[
26
]
). This definition does not preserve normal forms,
i.e.
{x}\,{y}
is a
\mathtt{v}
-normal form but its translated version

{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(\mathop{!}{x})}}\,{\mathop{!}{y}}
is not a
\mathtt{f}
-normal
form. We restore this fundamental property by using the well-known notion of
superdevelopment
[
10
]
, so that
\mathtt{d!}
-reductions are applied by
the translation on the fly. Moreover, simulation of CBN/CBV in the

\lambda!
-calculus also holds.

Lemma 32

(Simulation
[
12
]
)
.

Let
t\in\mathcal{T_{\lambda}}
.

1.

t\not\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}}
implies

{t}^{\mathtt{n}}\not\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
, and
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}}s
implies

{t}^{\mathtt{n}}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}{s}^{\mathtt{n}}
.

2.

t\not\rightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}
implies

{t}^{\mathtt{v}}\not\rightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}
, and
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}s
implies

{t}^{\mathtt{v}}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}{s}^{\mathtt{v}}
.

Example 33
.

Consider again
t_{0}={{{\tt K}}\,{({z}\,{{\tt I}})}}\,{({{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}})}
.
To illustrate the CBN case
(Lem.
32
:
1
), notice
that the reduction sequence
t_{0}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{cbn}}{z}\,{{\tt I}}=s_{0}

given in Ex.
12
is translated to the sequence
{t_{0}}^{\mathtt{n}}={{{\tt K}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}}\,{(\mathop{!}{({{\tt I}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}})})}=t^{\prime}_{0}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}}}={s_{0}}^{\mathtt{n}}
given in
Ex.
27
.

To illustrate the CBV case
(Lem.
32
:
2
),
consider the sequence
t_{0}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{cbv}}{x}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{{\tt I}}}]=s_{1}
in Ex.
26
. Then for

{\tt I}^{\prime}=\lambda{w}.{\mathop{!}{w}}
we have:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\enskip}l@{\enskip}l@{\enskip}l@{\enskip}l}{t_{0}}^{\mathtt{v}}&=&{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(\underline{{(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}})}\,{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}})}}\,{({{\tt I}^{\prime}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&{\underline{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({(\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}})}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}])}}}\,{({{\tt I}^{\prime}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}\\
&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{d!}}&\underline{{{(\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}})}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}]}\,{({{\tt I}^{\prime}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&{{(\mathop{!}{x})}[{y}\backslash{\underline{{{\tt I}^{\prime}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}}]}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}]\\
&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{dB}}&{\underline{{(\mathop{!}{x})}[{y}\backslash{{(\mathop{!}{w})}[{w}\backslash{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}]}]}}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}]&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{s!}}&{\underline{{(\mathop{!}{x})}[{w}\backslash{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}]}}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}]\\
&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{s!}}&{(\mathop{!}{x})}[{x}\backslash{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}]&=&{s_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}\end{array}

Notice how this sequence requires extra reduction steps with respect to the one
given in Ex.
26
. Indeed, the
\mathtt{e}
-step
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{d!}}
in
the
\lambda!
-calculus has no counterpart in CBV.

8
Typed Translations

Call-by-Name.

We study the correspondence between derivations in CBN and their encodings in
the
\lambda!
-calculus. First we inject the set of types for
\mathcal{N}

(generated by the base types
\mathtt{n}
and
\mathtt{a}
) into the set of
types of
\mathcal{B}
(generated also by the base type
\mathtt{vl}
) by means of
the function:
{\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{n}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}\mathtt{n}
,
{\mathtt{a}}^{\mathtt{n}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}\mathtt{a}
,
{({\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma})}^{\mathtt{n}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}{{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}
and

{[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}}^{\mathtt{n}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}[{\sigma_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}]_{i\in I}
. Then we translate terms, using the
function
{\_}^{\mathtt{n}}
from Sec.
7
. Translation of
contexts is defined as expected:
{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}}=\{{{x_{i}}:{{\mathcal{M}_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\}_{i\in I}
. Another notion is needed to
restrict
\mathcal{B}
derivations to those that come from the translation of
some
\mathcal{N}
derivation. Indeed, a
\mathcal{B}
derivation
\Phi
is

\mathtt{n}
-relevant
if all the contexts and types involved in
\Phi
are in
the image of the translation
{\_}^{\mathtt{n}}
. We then obtain:

Theorem 34
.

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

if and only if

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}

is
\mathtt{n}
-relevant.

Proof.

The left-to-right implication is by induction on
\Phi
by analysing the last
rule applied.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{N}}_{p}})}
. Then we have
t={r}\,{u}
,
\sigma=\mathtt{n}
and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}^{\prime}+1
, and
\Phi
is

\displaystyle\penalty{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{r}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{N}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}+1})}{{{r}\,{u}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

i.h.
on the premise we may construct
\Phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\penalty{{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 29.73865pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=59.4773pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}:{{\mathtt{vl}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}+1})}{{{{r}^{\mathtt{n}}}\,{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}:{{\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}

on all the premises we may construct
\Phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\penalty{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}};{x}:{[{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}]_{i\in I}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.88258pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty({{{\Delta_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}})_{i\in I}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=179.53268pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{+_{i\in I}{\Delta_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({+_{i\in I}\mathit{m}_{i}},{1+_{i\in I}\mathit{e}_{i}},{+_{i\in I}\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}:{[{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}]_{i\in I}}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{({{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x})}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{{\Delta_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime}+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}},{1+\mathit{e}^{\prime}+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime}+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}})}{{{{r}^{\mathtt{n}}}[{x}\backslash{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}]}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}

In all cases, the resulting derivation is
\mathtt{n}
-relevant by the
i.h.
and the
fact that the
\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
rule (in the last case) is used as required.

The right-to-left implication is by induction on
\Phi^{\prime}
by analysing the last
rule applied.

•

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}
. Then
t={t_{1}}\,{u}
with
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}={{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\,{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}
,
\Gamma={\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}
,
{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}=\mathtt{n}=\sigma
and
\Phi^{\prime}
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}:{\overline{\mathtt{a}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\mathrel{+}{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}+1})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}+1
. Moreover, since
{u}^{\mathtt{n}}
is typed with

\overline{\mathtt{a}}
, then the only possible case is
\mathtt{vl}
,
i.e.
{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}=\emptyset
(and thus
\Gamma_{2}=\emptyset
) and

\mathit{m}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{s}_{2}=0
. Then, by the
i.h.
on
\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}
we
have

{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}
.
Finally, we derive
\Phi
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{N}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+1})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}

We conclude since
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}
and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+1
.

•

\mathtt{({abs_{p}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=\lambda{x}.{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}
and
t=\lambda{x}.{u}
and since
\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{n}
-relevant, then it has a premise of
the form

{}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}};{x}:{{\mathtt{tt}_{0}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}-1})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\mathtt{tt}_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}
.
The
i.h.
gives

{}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma;{x}:{\mathtt{tt}_{0}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}-1})}{{t}:{\mathtt{tt}_{1}}}}
.
We thus conclude

{}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{a}}}}

by rule
\mathtt{({abs_{p}})}
.

•

\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=\mathop{!}{u}
, which is not
possible by definition. Hence this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(u)}
which is not
possible by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
with
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}={\mathop{!}{{r}^{\mathtt{n}}}}[{x}\backslash{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}]
,
\Gamma={\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}
and
\Phi^{\prime}
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}};{x}:{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}:{{\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\mathrel{+}{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}

with
\mathsf{tight}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
. This means in particular that

\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}
is typed with
\mathtt{n}
which is not possible
by construction. So this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=x
and
t=x
. We trivially
conclude with rule
\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
.

•

\mathtt{({app_{c}})}
. Then
t={t_{1}}\,{u}
with
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}={{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\,{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}
, and
\Gamma={\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}
. Since
\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{n}
-relevant, then it is of
the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{t_{1}}\,{\mathop{!}{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}

Then, by the
i.h.
on
\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}
there exists a type derivation

{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}}}}
.
On the other hand,
\Phi^{\prime}_{u}
necessarily commes from a rule
\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}
, and
thus there exist type derivations

{\Phi^{i}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma^{i}_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{i}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{i}_{2}},{\mathit{s}^{i}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\sigma_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}

with
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{n}}=[{\sigma_{i}}^{\mathtt{n}}]_{i\in I}
,

{\Gamma_{2}}^{\mathtt{n}}=+_{i\in I}{\Gamma^{i}_{2}}
,
\mathit{m}_{2}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}^{i}_{2}}
,

\mathit{e}_{2}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}^{i}_{2}}
and
\mathit{s}_{2}=+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}^{i}_{2}}
. We
conclude by applying
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{N}}_{c}})}
to derive
\Phi
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}\quad({{\Phi^{i}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma^{i}_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{i}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{i}_{2}},{\mathit{s}^{i}_{2}})}{{u}:{\sigma_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{N}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}

•

\mathtt{({appt_{c}})}
. This case is similar to the previous one, resorting to
the fact that
\mathsf{tight}({\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{n}})
implies
\mathsf{tight}(\mathcal{M})
.

•

\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=\lambda{x}.{{u}^{\mathtt{n}}}
and
t=\lambda{x}.{u}
and since
\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{n}
-relevant, then it has a premise of
the form

{}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}};{x}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\tau}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}
.
The
i.h.
gives

{}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma;{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\tau}}}
.
We thus conclude

{}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}}}}

by rule
\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}
.

•

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=\mathop{!}{u}
, which is not
possible by definition. Hence this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}=\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(t^{\prime})}
which is not
possible by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. This case is similar to
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
.

∎

This result is illustrated by the tight type derivations in Ex.
12

and
31
for the terms
t_{0}
and
t^{\prime}_{0}
resp. Moreover,
tightness is preserved by the translation of contexts and types, hence:

Corollary 35
.

If

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{N}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

is tight, then there exists
{p}\in\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}
such that
{t}^{\mathtt{n}}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps,
\mathit{e}

\mathtt{e}
-steps, and
|{p}|_{\mathtt{f}}=\mathit{s}
. Conversely, if

{{\Phi^{\prime}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\mathtt{n}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{n}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{n}}}}}

is tight and
\mathtt{n}
-relevant, then there exists
p\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{n}}
such that
t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{n}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps,
\mathit{e}

\mathtt{e}
-steps, and
|{p}|_{\mathtt{n}}=\mathit{s}
.

This result shows that not only from the tight type system
\mathcal{N}

it is possible to extract exact measures for the image of the
CBN in the
\lambda!
-calculus, but more interestingly, also that from the
tight type system
\mathcal{B}
for the
\lambda!
-calculus it is possible to
extract exact measures for CBN. In this sense, the goal of encoding tight
typing in a unified framework is achieved.

Call-by-Value.

In contrast with the CBN case, the set of type for system

\mathcal{V}
is not a subset of that for
\mathcal{B}
, and we need to
properly translate types. To that end, we introduce two
mutually dependent translations
{\_}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}
and
{\_}^{\mathtt{v}}
:

\begin{array}[]{c@{\qquad}c}\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\mathtt{tt}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\mathtt{tt}&\text{if $\mathtt{tt}\neq\mathtt{vr}$}\\
{\mathtt{vr}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\mathtt{n}\\
{({\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma})}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}\\
{([\sigma]_{i\in I})}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&[{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}]_{i\in I}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\mathtt{tt}}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&\mathtt{tt}&\text{if $\mathtt{tt}\neq\mathtt{vr}$}\\
{\mathtt{vr}}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&[\mathtt{n}]\\
{({\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma})}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}\\
{([\sigma]_{i\in I})}^{\mathtt{v}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}&[{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}]_{i\in I}\end{array}\end{array}

Remark that
{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}
for every multitype
\mathcal{M}
.
Translation
{\_}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}
for a context
\Gamma
is defined as expected:

{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}=\{{{x_{i}}:{{\mathcal{M}_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}\}_{i\in I}
. To
translate terms, we resort to the function
{\_}^{\mathtt{v}}
defined in
Sec.
7
. We also restrict
\mathcal{B}
derivations to
those that come from the translation of some
\mathcal{V}
derivation. Indeed,
a
\mathcal{B}
derivation
\Phi
is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant
if:

(1)

all the contexts and types involved in
\Phi

are in the image of the translations
{\_}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}

and
{\_}^{\mathtt{v}}
respectively; and

(2)

rule
\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}
is only applied to terms having a type of the form

[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\tau}]
.

To state the preservation of typing derivations between systems
\mathcal{V}

and
\mathcal{B}
we define measures over type derivations in both systems to
conveniently capture the relationship between the exponential steps in the
source and the target derivation. The intuition is that we need to compensate
for those
\mathtt{d!}
-redexes of the
\lambda!
-calculus that might be introduced
when translating. For all the typing rules with two premises, we write

\Phi_{t}
and
\Phi_{u}
for the first and second premise respectively. The
first measure for system
\mathcal{V}
is given by induction on
\Phi
as
follows:

(1)

for
\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}1
;

(2)

for
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
and
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
,

\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}0
;

(3)

for
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
,
\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{t}}\right)+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)-1
if
\mathsf{val}(t)
;

(4)

for
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
and
\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
,
\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{t}}\right)+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)+1
if
\neg\mathsf{val}(t)
; and

(5)

in any other case
\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
is defined as the sum of the
recursive calls over all premises.

The second measure for system
\mathcal{B}
is also defined by induction on

\Phi
as follows:

(1)

for
\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}
and
\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
,
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}0
;

(2)

for
\mathtt{({app_{p}})}
,
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{t}}\right)+\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)-1
if
\mathsf{val}(t)
;

(3)

for
\mathtt{({app_{c}})}
and
\mathtt{({appt_{c}})}
,
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi}\right)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textit{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}$}}\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{t}}\right)+\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)+1
if
\neg\mathsf{val}(t)
; and

(4)

in any other case
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
is defined as the sum of the
recursive calls over all premises.

Then, we obtain:

Theorem 36
.

1.

If

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}
,
then

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant with
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
.

2.

If

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant, then

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

with
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
.

Proof.

1.

By induction on
\Phi
by analysing the last rule applied.

•

\mathtt{({var_{p}})}
. Then

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{vr}}}}

and
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{!}{x}
. We conclude by applying
\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
and

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
, obtaining

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{x}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}}
,
which is trivially
\mathtt{v}
-relevant. The counters are as expected since

\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)=1
.

•

\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
. Then

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

and
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{!}{x}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}
with

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{x}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}
,
which is trivially
\mathtt{v}
-relevant. Note that the counters are as expected since

\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)=0
.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}

and
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{!}{\lambda{x}.{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}}
. We conclude by

\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}
with

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{\lambda{x}.{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}
,
which is trivially
\mathtt{v}
-relevant. Note that the counters are as expected since

\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)=0
.

•

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
,
\sigma=\mathtt{n}

and
\Phi
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}+1})}{{{r}\,{u}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
. By the
i.h.
we have
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivations

{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

and

{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

with
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)
. Moreover, since
u
is typed with
\overline{\mathtt{vr}}
,
then it is typed with
\mathtt{n}
or
\mathtt{vl}
, and thus

{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}^{\mathtt{v}}=\overline{\mathtt{a}}
. There are three
possible cases for
r
:

(a)

r={\mathtt{L}}\langle{x}\rangle
. Then
\overline{\mathtt{vl}}=\mathtt{vr}

with
{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}^{\mathtt{v}}=[\mathtt{n}]
.
Moreover, by definition
{r}^{\mathtt{v}}={{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{x}}\rangle
. Thus, we have

{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{x}}\rangle}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}}
.
With a simple induction on
\mathtt{L}
we can show that there exists a

\mathtt{v}
-relevant type derivation

{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{x}\rangle}:{\mathtt{n}}}}
,
that results from removing the occurrence of rule
\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
in the base
case. Finally, we conclude by applying
\mathtt{({app_{p}})}
to derive a

\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{x}\rangle}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\overline{\mathtt{a}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-1+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)-1+\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
as expected.

(b)

r={\mathtt{L}}\langle{{r^{\prime}}\,{r^{\prime\prime}}}\rangle
. Then, we have

{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}
.
We conclude by applying
\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}
and

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}
to derive
\Phi^{\prime}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=101.74438pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 1.91205pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({r}^{\mathtt{v}})}}:{\mathtt{n}}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\overline{\mathtt{a}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}
. Hence,
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
as expected. Notice that
\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant by the
i.h.
and the fact that no
\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}
rule was added in the final
step.

(c)

r={\mathtt{L}}\langle{\lambda{x}.{r^{\prime}}}\rangle
. Then, it is necessarily
the case that
\Phi
has a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant subderivation for a judgement of
the form

{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}},{\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r^{\prime}}}:{\overline{\mathtt{vl}}}}

for some appropriate
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{m}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathit{e}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathit{s}^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
\overline{\mathtt{vl}}
is
\mathtt{vr}
or
\mathtt{n}
, this
leads to a contradiction because abstractions cannot be assigned type

\mathtt{vr}
or
\mathtt{n}
in system
\mathcal{V}
. Hence, this case
does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
and
\Phi
is of the
form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}[{x}\backslash{u}]}:{\sigma}}

with
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma_{1}(x))
and
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}
. By the
i.h.
on

\Phi_{u}
there exists a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}

with
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)
. By the
i.h.
again there exists
a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

with
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)
. Thus, we conclude by applying

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
with
\Phi^{\prime}_{r}
,
\Phi^{\prime}_{u}
and
\mathsf{tight}({\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}(x))
,
hence obtaining the
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

with
\mathcal{M}=[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
and

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
, deriving a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\left(\hskip-10.00002pt\raisebox{-10.00002pt}{
\enskip{
\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 40.48793pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=80.97586pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({var_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{x}:{[{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty
}\hskip-5.0pt\right)_{i\in I}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{x}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{x}}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}

Note that the counters are as expected since
\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)=0
. We
conclude since
{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}
.

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t={r}\,{u}
and
\Phi
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}\,{u}}:{\sigma}}

where
\mathit{m}=\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1
,
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1
,
and
\mathit{s}=\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}
. By the
i.h.
on
\Phi_{u}
there exists a

\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

with
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)
. And by the
i.h.
on
\Phi_{r}

there exists a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{[{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}]}}}

with
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)
. We should consider two cases
based on the shape of
r
:

(a)

r={\mathtt{L}}\langle{v}\rangle
. Then
{r}^{\mathtt{v}}={{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle
and
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}={{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{r^{\prime}}\rangle}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}
. With a simple
induction on
\mathtt{L}
we can show from
\Phi^{\prime}_{r}
that there exists a

\mathtt{v}
-relevant type derivation

{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{r^{\prime}}\rangle}:{{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}}
,
that results from removing the occurrence of rule
\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
in the base
case. Finally, since
{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}
, we conclude by
applying
\mathtt{({app_{c}})}
with
\Phi^{\prime\prime}
and
\Phi_{u}
to derive a

\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-1+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)-1+\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
as expected.

(b)

r\neq{\mathtt{L}}\langle{v}\rangle
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}={\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({r}^{\mathtt{v}})}}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}
. Since
{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}
,
we conclude by deriving
\Phi^{\prime}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{[{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}]}}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=131.6058pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.68983pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({r}^{\mathtt{v}})}}:{{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}_{2}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}+1+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
as expected. Notice that the
resulting derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{v}
-relevantsince the added
\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}

rule acts on a multiset of the required form.

\mathtt{({appt^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. This case is similar to the previous one.

•

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
. Then
t=\lambda{x}.{r}
and
\Phi
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty({{\Phi_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{i}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{r}:{\tau_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{{\Gamma_{i}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}}\vdash^{({+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}},{1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}},{+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}})}{{\lambda{x}.{r}}:{[{\Gamma_{i}(x)}\to{\tau_{i}}]_{i\in I}}}

where
\mathit{e}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}
. By the
i.h.
there exists a
derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}
,
which is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant, where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathit{e}_{i}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{i}}\right)
for
each
i\in I
. We then conclude by applying
\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}
and

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
, hence deriving a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
as
follows

\displaystyle\penalty\left(\hskip-10.00002pt\raisebox{-10.00002pt}{
\enskip{
\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 21.8195pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\vskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=147.54845pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{\lambda{x}.{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}}:{{{\Gamma_{i}(x)}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}$}}}}\enskip\penalty
}\hskip-5.0pt\right)_{i\in I}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\mathrel{\setminus\!\!\setminus}{x}}}\vdash^{({+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}},{1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}}},{+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{[{\Gamma_{i}(x)}\to{\tau_{i}}]_{i\in I}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}

taking
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}}=1+_{i\in I}{(\mathit{e}_{i}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi_{i}}\right))}=\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)
as
expected.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. This case is similar to
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
.

1.

By induction on
\Phi^{\prime}
analysing the last rule applied.

•

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}
. Then
t={t_{1}}\,{u}
with
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}={r}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}
for some term
r
,
\Gamma={\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}
,
{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathtt{n}=\sigma
and
\Phi^{\prime}
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\overline{\mathtt{a}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\mathrel{+}{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}+1})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}
. Moreover, since
{u}^{\mathtt{v}}
is
typed with
\overline{\mathtt{a}}
, then it is typed with
\mathtt{n}
or

\mathtt{vl}
,
i.e.
{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}^{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, by the
i.h.
on

\Phi^{\prime}_{u}
we have

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)
. There are two possible
cases for
{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}
and, hence, for
r
:

(a)

{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}={{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle
(
i.e.
\mathsf{val}(t_{1})
). Then,
r={{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{r^{\prime}}\rangle
. Moreover, by a
simple induction on
\mathtt{L}
, from
\Phi^{\prime}_{r}
we get the
\mathtt{v}
-relevant type derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}}

such that
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\right)=\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)
. Note that

[\mathtt{n}]={\mathtt{vr}}^{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, by the
i.h.
we get

{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{\mathtt{vr}}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{1}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)
. Finally, we conclude by applying
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
to
derive
\Phi
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{\mathtt{vr}}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+1+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+1+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-(\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)-1)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
as expected.

(b)

{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}\neq{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle
(
i.e.
\neg\mathsf{val}(t_{1})
). Then,
r=\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}})}
. Since
\Phi^{\prime}
is

\mathtt{v}
-relevant, it is necessarily the case where
\Phi^{\prime}_{r}
comes from an
application of rule
\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}
. Moreover,
\mathtt{n}={\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{v}}
, hence we have a
\mathtt{v}
-relevant type derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

satisfying the hypothesis of the theorem. By the
i.h.
we get a type
derivation

{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{1}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)
. We conclude by applying
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
to derive

\Phi

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{\mathtt{n}}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\overline{\mathtt{vr}}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-(\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right))=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
as expected.

\mathtt{({abs_{p}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\lambda{x}.{t^{\prime}}
which is not
possible by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.
