\mathtt{({bg_{p}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{!}{t^{\prime}}
,
{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathtt{vl}=\sigma
and

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{t^{\prime}}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}
.
There are two possible cases: either

(1)

t=x=t^{\prime}
; or

(2)

t=\lambda{x}.{u}
with
t^{\prime}=\lambda{x}.{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}
.

We resort to
\mathtt{({val_{p}})}
or
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}
resp. to conclude with

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{t}:{\mathtt{vl}}}}
.
Note that the counters are as expected since
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)=0
.

•

\mathtt{({dr_{p}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(t^{\prime})}
which is not
possible by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
. Then
t={r}[{x}\backslash{u}]
with
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}={{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}[{x}\backslash{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}]
,
\Gamma={\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}
and
\Phi^{\prime}
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}};{x}:{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\mathtt{n}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

\mathtt{({es_{p}})}

\displaystyle{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\mathrel{+}{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}

with
\mathsf{tight}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}
. Moreover, since

\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant,
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is of the form
{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}
. Then, by the

i.h.
there exists type derivations

{\Phi_{r}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1};{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{\sigma}}}

and

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathtt{n}}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{1}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)
and
\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)
resp. Finally, since
\mathsf{tight}({\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}})
implies

\mathsf{tight}(\mathcal{M})
, we conclude by applying rule
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
with
\Phi_{r}
,

\Phi_{u}
obtaining the type derivation

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=x
which is not possible by
definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({app_{c}})}
. Then
t={t_{1}}\,{u}

with
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}={r}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}
for some term
r
,
\Gamma={\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}
. Moreover, since
\Phi^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{v}
-relevant,
then it is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{r}:{{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}}\quad{\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{2}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}}

\mathtt{({app_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{+}{\Gamma_{2}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}+\mathit{m}_{2}+1},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{1}+\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{{r}\,{{u}^{\mathtt{v}}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}
. Moreover, by definition

{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, by the
i.h.
on
\Phi^{\prime}_{u}
there
exists a type derivation

{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{2}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{u}}\right)
. We should consider two
cases based on the shape of
{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}
and, hence, of
r
:

(a)

{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}={{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle
(
i.e.
\mathsf{val}(t_{1})
). Then,
r={{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{r^{\prime}}\rangle
. Moreover, by a
simple induction on
\mathtt{L}
, from
\Phi^{\prime}_{r}
we get

{\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{{\mathtt{L}}^{\mathtt{v}}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle}:{[{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}]}}}

\mathtt{v}
-relevant such that
\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\right)=\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)
.
Note that

[{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}]={[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}^{\mathtt{v}}
. Then, by the
i.h.
we get

{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{1}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{t_{1}}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi_{r}}\right)
. We conclude by applying
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
to derive

\Phi
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}+1},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}

where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+1+\mathit{e}_{2}-1=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-(\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right))=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
as expected.

(b)

{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}\neq{\mathtt{L}}\langle{\mathop{!}{r^{\prime}}}\rangle
(
i.e.
\neg\mathsf{val}(t_{1})
). Then,
r=\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}})}
. Moreover,

\Phi^{\prime}_{r}
necessarily comes from an application of rule
\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}
, and

[{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\to{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}]={[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}^{\mathtt{v}}
. Thus, we have a

\mathtt{v}
-relevant type derivation

{\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{1}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{{t_{1}}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

satisfying the hypothesis of the theorem. By the
i.h.
we get a type
derivation

{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}

with
\mathit{e}_{1}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}}\right)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)
. We conclude by applying
\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
to derive

\Phi

\displaystyle\penalty{\Phi_{t_{1}}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{1}},{\mathit{e}_{1}},{\mathit{s}_{1}})}{{t_{1}}:{[{\mathcal{M}}\to{\sigma}]}}}\quad{\Phi_{u}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{2}},{\mathit{e}_{2}},{\mathit{s}_{2}})}{{u}:{\mathcal{M}}}}

\mathtt{({app^{\mathcal{V}}_{p}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\mathtt{n}}}

where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{e}_{1}+\mathit{e}_{2}-1=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)-1=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{1}+\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{2}-(\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{r}}\right)+\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{u}}\right)+1)=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({appt_{c}})}
. This case is similar to the previous one, resorting to
the fact that
\mathsf{tight}({\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}})
implies
\mathsf{tight}(\mathcal{M})
.

•

\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\lambda{x}.{t^{\prime}}
which is not
possible by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{!}{t^{\prime}}
,
\Gamma={}\mathrel{+_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{i}}
,
\sigma=[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}
and

\Phi^{\prime}
is of the form

\displaystyle\penalty({{\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{t^{\prime}}:{{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}}})_{i\in I}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}},{1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}}},{+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}})}{{\mathop{!}{t^{\prime}}}:{[{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}]_{i\in I}}}

where
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}}
. There are two possible cases for

t^{\prime}
:

(a)

t^{\prime}=x
(
i.e.
t=x
). Then,
\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
necessarily comes from

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}
for each
i\in I
,
i.e.
({{\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{x}:{[{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}}})_{i\in I}
.
Hence,
\Gamma={x}:{[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}}
,
\mathit{m}=\mathit{s}=0
and
\mathit{e}^{\prime}=1
. We conclude by
\mathtt{({var^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
with

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{{x}:{[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{x}:{[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}}}}
,
since
{\Gamma(x)}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}=[{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}]_{i\in I}={\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}
. Note that the counters are as expected since

\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)=0
.

(b)

t^{\prime}=\lambda{x}.{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}
(
i.e.
t=\lambda{x}.{r}
). Note
that, for every
i\in I
,
{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}=\mathtt{a}
leads to a
contradiction, since
\mathtt{a}
is not in the image of the translation.
Hence,
\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
necessarily comes from
\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}
,
i.e.
\sigma_{i}={\mathcal{M}_{i}}\to{\tau_{i}}
and
\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
is

\displaystyle\penalty{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}};{x}:{{\mathcal{M}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\tau_{i}}^{\mathtt{v}}}}

\mathtt{({abs_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{\Gamma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{\lambda{x}.{{r}^{\mathtt{v}}}}:{{\sigma_{i}}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}}

for each
i\in I
. Then, by the
i.h.
on we get

({{\Phi_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{i};{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{r}:{\tau_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

with
({\mathit{e}_{i}=\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\right)})_{i\in I}
. We
conclude by applying rule
\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}
to derive
\Phi
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty({{\Phi_{i}}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma_{i};{x}:{\mathcal{M}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}_{i}},{\mathit{e}_{i}},{\mathit{s}_{i}})}{{r}:{\tau_{i}}}}})_{i\in I}

\mathtt{({abs^{\mathcal{V}}_{c}})}

\displaystyle{\Gamma}\vdash^{({+_{i\in I}{\mathit{m}_{i}}},{1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}},{+_{i\in I}{\mathit{s}_{i}}})}{{t}:{[{\mathcal{M}_{i}}\to{\tau_{i}}]_{i\in I}}}

where
\mathit{e}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}_{i}}=1+_{i\in I}{(\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\right))}=1+_{i\in I}{\mathit{e}^{\prime}_{i}}-(+_{i\in I}{\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}_{i}}\right)})=\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)
as expected.

•

\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}
. Then
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}=\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{(t^{\prime})}
which is not
possible by definition. Hence, this case does not apply.

•

\mathtt{({es_{c}})}
. This case is similar to
\mathtt{({es_{p}})}
.

∎

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{x}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}:{{[\,]}\to{[\mathtt{n}]}}}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{2},{0})}{{\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}}}:{{[\mathtt{n}]}\to{[{[\,]}\to{[\mathtt{n}]}]}}}

\mathtt{({var_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{z}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathtt{({bg_{c}})}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}:{\mathtt{vl}}}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{1})}{{{z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}}}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({1},{2},{1})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}})}\,{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}}:{[{[\,]}\to{[\mathtt{n}]}]}}

\mathtt{({dr_{c}})}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({1},{2},{1})}{{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}})}\,{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})})}}:{{[\,]}\to{[\mathtt{n}]}}}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{w}}:{[\,]}}

\displaystyle{{z}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({3},{4},{1})}{{{\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}})}\,{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})})}}\,{({{\tt I}^{\prime}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}

Type derivation in System
\mathcal{B}
for
{t_{0}}^{\mathtt{v}}={({{{\tt K}}\,{({z}\,{{\tt I}})}}\,{({{\tt I}}\,{{\tt I}})})}^{\mathtt{v}}={\mathop{\textnormal{der}}{({(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{\lambda{y}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}})}\,{({z}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})})}}\,{({{\tt I}^{\prime}}\,{\mathop{!}{{\tt I}^{\prime}}})}
,
with
{\tt I}^{\prime}=\lambda{w}.{\mathop{!}{w}}
.

Fig.
8
illustrates this result over the term
t_{0}
in
Ex.
26
. As a further (simpler) example, consider the CBV term

{(\lambda{x}.{x})}\,{y}
(whose derivation
\Phi
is on the left) and
its translation
{(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{x}})}\,{\mathop{!}{y}}
into the

\lambda!
-calculus (whose derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
is on the right):

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{vr}}}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\lambda{x}.{x}}:{[{[\mathtt{vr}]}\to{\mathtt{vr}}]}}

\displaystyle{{y}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{y}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}

\displaystyle{{y}:{[\mathtt{vr}]}}\vdash^{({1},{1},{0})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{x})}\,{y}}:{\mathtt{vr}}}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{x}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\displaystyle{{x}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{x}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}

\displaystyle{}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{x}}}:{{[\mathtt{n}]}\to{[\mathtt{n}]}}}

\displaystyle{{y}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{0},{0})}{{y}:{\mathtt{n}}}

\displaystyle{{y}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({0},{1},{0})}{{\mathop{!}{y}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}

\displaystyle{{y}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}\vdash^{({1},{2},{0})}{{{(\lambda{x}.{\mathop{!}{x}})}\,{\mathop{!}{y}}}:{[\mathtt{n}]}}

Notice that
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
if and only if
\mathsf{tight}({\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}})
.
Unfortunately, this is not sufficient to translate a tight
derivation in
\mathcal{V}
into a tight derivation in
\mathcal{B}
. Indeed,
the variable
x
of type
\mathtt{vr}
translates to
\mathop{!}{x}
of type

[\mathtt{n}]
. However, this only happens
if the derived type is
\mathtt{vr}
, which is an auxiliary type of system

\mathcal{V}
used to identify variables that are not used as values because
they will be applied to some argument. In the case of

\mathtt{f}
-relevant
\mathcal{V}
derivations
, defined as not deriving type
\mathtt{vr}
,
tightness is indeed preserved. As a consequence, as for CBN, is it possible to
study CBV in the unified framework of the
\lambda!
-calculus and extract exact
measures for it by resorting to relevant tight derivations:

Corollary 37
.

If

{\Phi}\rhd_{\mathcal{V}}{{\Gamma}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}},{\mathit{s}})}{{t}:{\sigma}}}

is tight and
\mathtt{f}
-relevant, then there
exists
{p}\in\mathsf{no}_{{\tt scf}}
such that
{t}^{\mathtt{v}}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{f}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps,
\mathit{e}+\mathtt{e}\!\left({\Phi}\right)

\mathtt{e}
-steps, and
|{p}|_{\mathtt{f}}=\mathit{s}
. Conversely, if

{\Phi^{\prime}}\rhd_{\mathcal{B}}{{{\Gamma}^{\overline{\mathtt{v}}}}\vdash^{({\mathit{m}},{\mathit{e}^{\prime}},{\mathit{s}})}{{{t}^{\mathtt{v}}}:{{\sigma}^{\mathtt{v}}}}}

is tight and
\mathtt{v}
-relevant, then there exists
p\in\mathsf{no}_{\mathtt{v}}
such that

t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathtt{v}}^{(\mathit{m},\mathit{e})}p
with
\mathit{m}

\mathtt{m}
-steps,

\mathit{e}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathtt{e}}\!\left({\Phi^{\prime}}\right)

\mathtt{e}
-steps, and
|{p}|_{\mathtt{v}}=\mathit{s}
.

9
Conclusion

Following recent works exploring the power of CBPV, we develop a technique for
deriving tight type systems for CBN/CBV as special cases of a single tight type
system for the
\lambda!
-calculus, a subcalculus of CBPV inspired by Linear
Logic.

The idea to study semantical and operational properties in a CBPV framework in
order to transfer them to CBN/CBV has so far be
exploited in different
works
[
41
,
25
,
26
,
32
,
16
,
12
,
45
]
.
Moreover, relational models for CBN/CBV can be derived from the relational
model for CBPV, resulting in non-idempotent intersection type systems for them,
that provide upper bounds for the length of normalization
sequences
[
12
]
. However, the challenging quest of a (tight)
quantitative type system for CBV, giving
exact measures
for the
length of normalization sequences instead of
upper bounds
, and being at
the same time encodable in CBPV, has been open. None of the existing
proposals
[
3
,
39
]
could be defined/explained within such an
approach. In particular, the tight type systems that we propose for CBN/CBV
give independent exact measures for the length of multiplicative and
exponential reduction to normal form, as well as the size of these normal
forms.

Different topics deserve future attention. One of them is the study of

strong
reduction for the
\lambda!
-calculus, which allows to reduce
terms under
all
the constructors, including bang. Appropriate
encodings of strong CBN and strong CBV should follow. Linear (head) reduction,
as well as other more sophisticated semantics like GOI also deserve some
attention.The tight systems presented in this work could also be used to
understand bounded computation.
